Whereas in my judgment, the public interest requires that Sims Ely, City Manager of Boulder City, Nevada, who, during the current month, will reach the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service, applicable to him, be exempted from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year:

### T05c123d1298db0801a3b2ed2

Text hash 83660b31d2eb815b93587efe5ad2006b47639c01d4b3556c412464b34d210a51; absolute characters 0–707; files: jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-8073-exemption-newton-c-lammond-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt

```text
Whereas, in my judgment, the public interest requires that Newton C. Lammond, senior clerk, Office of the Judge Advocate General, War Department, who, during the month of June, 1939, will reach the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service, applicable to him, be exempted from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year:

Now, Therefore, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me by section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), I hereby exempt the said Newton C. Lammond from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending June 30, 1940.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

The White House,

March 23, 1939.

```

Executive Order
PROPERTY OF
APR 151939
YTRVARD LAW LIBRARY
EXEMPTION OF SIMS ELY FROM COMPULSORY RETIREMENT FOR AGE
WHEREAS in my judgment , the public interest requires that Sims Ely, City
Manager of Boulder City , Nevada , who, during the current month, will reach the
retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service , applicable to
him, be exempted from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year :
NOW, THEREFORE , by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me
by section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932 , 47 Stat . 382 , 404 ( U.

```text
Instrument: EO:8293
Source: https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-1939-12-05/pdf/FR-1939-12-05.pdf
SHA-256 of the downloaded file: 88db380459f9b4ebf3d38e9eb67cfccebeda431794e04bdc2facd9920d28735b
Location: 4 FR 4762, PDF pages 2-3 (confidence high, from the pdfplumber recheck)
Reading-order text rebuilt from the scan's OCR word positions (pdfplumber), from the order's heading to its filing line. May contain recognition errors, and page furniture printed in between (running heads are left out; a masthead or contents column may remain). Not an authenticated transcription: check the PDF pages.

=== PDF page 2 ===
EXECUTIVE ORDER
Suspension of Eight-Hour Law as to
Persons Employed by the Govern­
ment in the Construction of Cer­
tain Emergency Air Bases
WHEREAS the act of April 25, 1939,
53 Stat. 590, authorizes the Secretary of
of the Navy to establish, develop, or in­
crease naval aviation facilities at or in
the vicinity of certain places mentioned
therein, including Midway Island and
Wake Island; and
WHEREAS the Corps of Engineers,
United States Army, is engaged in harbor
development at Midway Island and Wake
il F.R. 1624.
=== PDF page 3 ===
Island pursuant to the act of August 26,
1937, 50 Stat. 844, by use of Government
forces, and has been requested by the
Secretary of the Navy to perform addi­
tional work necessary for the establish­
ment of naval aviation facilities at such
places; and
WHEREAS by section 1 of the act of
August 1, 1892, 27 Stat. 340, as amended
by the act of March 3, 1913, 37 Stat. 726
(UJS.C., title 40, sec. 321), the service and
employment of all laborers and mechan­
ics employed by the Government upon
any public work of the United States,
and of all persons employed by the Gov­
ernment to perform services similar to
those of laborers and mechanics in con­
nection with dredging or rock excavation
in any river or harbor of the United
States, is limited to eight hours in any
one calendar day except in case of extra­
ordinary emergency ; and
WHEREAS it appears that the inter­
ests of the national defense require the
establishment of naval aviation facilities
at the said Midway and Wake Islands at
the earliest practicable date; and
WHEREAS it further appears that
because of the isolation of such places
from sources of labor supply it will be
impossible to accomplish the work
required in the establishment of
such facilities within the time re­
quired by the interests of the national
defense unless the said eight-hour lim­
itation is suspended as to persons em­
ployed by the Government upon such
work; and
WHEREAS I find that by reason of
the foregoing an extraordinary emer­
gency exists:
NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the
authority vested in me by section 1 of
the said act of August 1, 1892, as
amended by the said act of March 3,
1913, and as President of the United
States, I hereby suspend the above-
mentioned provisions of law prohibiting
more than eight hours labor in any
one day of persons employed by the
Government of the United States as
to all work authorized and performed
under the said acts of August 26, 1937,
and April 25, 1939, at or in the vicinity
of Midway Island and Wake Island.
Franklin D Roosevelt
The White House,
November 30, 1939.
[No. 82931
[F. R. Doc. 39-4474; Filed, December 1, 1939;

```

Whereas, in my judgment, the public interest requires that Clarence E. Ingling, Chief of Files, The White House Office, Executive Office of the President, who was exempted from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year by Executive Order No. 8670 of February 4, 1941, be further exempted from such compulsory retirement for a period of one year:

Ingling from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending April 30, 1943.

FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT

The White House,

January 23, 1942.

```

```text
Whereas, in my judgment, the public interest requires that Fred C. Bailey, Fleet Manager, Maintenance and Repair Division, United States Maritime Commission, who, during the current month, will reach the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service, applicable to him, be exempted from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year:

Now, Therefore, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me by section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), I hereby exempt the said Fred C. Bailey from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending March 31, 1940.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

The White House,

March 7, 1939.

```

### Ta56b58a0fda0f3afdc79dd2a

```text
Whereas, in my judgment, the public interest requires that George K. Larrison, Hawaiian Homes Representative, Office of the Secretary, Division of Territories and Island Possessions, Department of the Interior, who, during the current month, will reach the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service, applicable to him, be exempted from compulsory retirement for age for a period of three months:

Now, Therefore, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me by section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), I hereby exempt the said George K. Larrison from compulsory retirement for age for a period of three months ending June 30, 1939.

FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT

The White House,

March 25, 1939.

```

The White House,

January 25, 1939.

Executive Order
OF
MAY 9-1939
HARVARD LAW LIBRARY
EXEMPTION OF ZEKE JOHNSON FROM COMPULSORY RETIREMENT FOR
AGE
WHEREAS, in my judgment, the public interest requires that Zeke Johnson ,
custodian , Natural Bridges National Monument , National Park Service , Department
of the Interior , who , during the current month, will reach the retirement age pre￾scribed for automatic separation from the service , applicable to him , be exempted
from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year:
NOW, THEREFORE , by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me
by section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932 , 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.
```

### Tec64e32d7b477d32fdf1e3a8

Text hash de91d0d80126ebd01f866f01ced81ca7abf8598bad0d5dd3dd2076798bef83c2; absolute characters 0–731; files: jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-8615-exemption-maurice-emerson-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt

```text
Whereas, in my judgment, the public interest requires that Maurice A. Emerson, Chief, Division of Paper Custody, Bureau of the Public Debt, Treasury Department, who, during the month of January, 1941, will reach the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service, applicable to him, be exempted from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year:

Now, Therefore, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me by section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), I hereby exempt the said Maurice A. Emerson from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending January 31, 1942.

FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT

The White House,

December 19, 1940.

```

Whereas, in my judgment, the public interest requires that Burt W. Andrews, principal attorney, Bureau of Internal Revenue, Treasury Department, who, during the current month, will reach the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service, applicable to him, be exempted from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year:

Now, Therefore, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me by section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), I hereby exempt the said Burt W. Andrews from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending November 30, 1942.

FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT

The White House,

November 25, 1941.

```

### Tff475e9708333aa9d135220c

Text hash 6e92a5e6634cd5ad75db90142aca90049a34dce15d7e551247801a115e781cd3; absolute characters 0–784; files: jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-8328-exemption-henry-clarke-hill-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt

```text
Whereas, in my judgment, the public interest requires that Henry Clarke Hill, Warden of the United States Penitentiary, Lewisburg, Pennsylvania, who was exempted from compulsory retirement for age for a period not in excess of one year from March 1, 1939, by Executive Order No. 8041 of January 25, 1939, be further exempted from compulsory retirement for a period not in excess of six months from March 1, 1940:

Now, Therefore, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me by section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), I hereby further exempt the said Henry Clarke Hill from compulsory retirement for age for a period not in excess of six months from March 1, 1940.

FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT

The White House,

January 22, 1940.

```

Page check per jobs/claim-kit/AGENT_PROTOCOL.md (working-text paragraph), done 2026-11-04: the APP mirror page record with the same sha256 in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl (Common Crawl capture 2025-10-13T08:25:15Z) has the heading \"Executive Order 8801—Exemption of Archie W. Davis From Compulsory Retirement for Age\", its date (1941-06-24) and subject agree with the held text, and its text_sha256 equals the held file's. Identity: the register lists this number with date 1941-06-24, signer Franklin D. Roosevelt and the title \"Exemption of Archie W. Davis From Compulsory Retirement for Age\"; the held text concerns Archie W. Davis and ends with the signature \"FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT\" and the date line \"June 24, 1941.\". Clauses are quoted from that registered file, not from the unregistered clause-drafts copy.\nContent: the order has one recital and one operative sentence. It exempts one named employee, Archie W. Davis (assistant mail, file and record clerk, Immigration and Naturalization Service, Department of Justice), from compulsory retirement for age under section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), for a period of one year ending July 31, 1942. The recital says he \"will reach the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service\" and asks that he \"be exempted from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year\". The inventory is therefore one clause, the exemption. Extension reading: this order is the initial exemption; any later exemption of the same person would be a new exercise of the section 204 power with its own period and a later event, not an end of this order, and the packet's searches show none in the held texts.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No earlier end found. The only cessation source is the order's own period, \\\"one year ending July 31, 1942\\\". The packet's bounded number search of the held order texts (lookup scope: exactly held_texts(), clause-drafts excluded) found no other held text that cites Executive Order 8801. The Federal Register catalog disposition-note search (later-events:ofr_catalog, checked 2026-10-19) returned no hit for this order. The person-name search of the held order texts (namier:held_texts_person_name, checked 2026-10-28, whose leads list later or undated orders only) found no later order naming Archie W. Davis. Statute check (the type rule research/readiness/type-rules/TR-RETIREMENT-EXEMPTION.json lists a change in the governing retirement statute as a possible earlier end): held APP exemption orders signed after this one still rest on the same section, for example EO 9271 of November 17, 1942 (\\\"section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), I hereby further exempt the said George T. Summerlin from compulsory retirement for age\\\") and EO 9530 of March 14, 1945 (\\\"section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), I hereby exempt the said Harry F. Hawley from compulsory retirement for age\\\"). Their wording shows that section 204 was still the authority for exemptions after this order's last day, so it had not been repealed. No held source shows that any amendment of section 204 extended or extinguished this order's dated exemption, and a later general rule is an end only with a reason it extinguished the command (owner ruling 2026-10-28). The employee's separation, retirement or death would end the practical use of the exemption but is not an instrument ending the command, and none is cited in the packet. Amendments are later events, not ends, and none is found.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"None found. The order contains one command, the exemption of one named employee; it prescribes no report, procedure, delegation, appropriation or continuing duty. The compulsory-retirement rule it excepts comes from section 204 and the retirement statutes, which are independent of this order, so when the exemption's period ended no command of this order remained to continue.\",\"savings_check\":\"The exemption is exhausted at 1942-07-31; its accrued effects (service, pay, retirement credit) are separate from the order and are stated as transferred_or_accrued. No later exemption order for the same person is found by the name search.\",\"scope_check\":\"Scope: one named employee, Archie W. Davis (assistant mail, file and record clerk, Immigration and Naturalization Service, Department of Justice). The dossier inventory has one whole-order clause (characters 0-700 of the registered file, the recital and the operative sentence); the signature and date line follow it in the full 735-character text. grade mirror_transcription, acceptable_as_original false; standing note: original not yet held; replace and re-check quotes when an official copy is retrieved). Page check per jobs/claim-kit/AGENT_PROTOCOL.md (working-text paragraph), done 2026-11-04: the APP mirror page record with the same sha256 in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl (Common Crawl capture 2025-09-17T06:13:59Z) has the heading \"Executive Order 8899—Exemption of Edward D. Anderson From Compulsory Retirement for Age\", its date (1941-09-15) and subject agree with the held text, and its text_sha256 equals the held file's. Identity: the register lists this number with date 1941-09-15, signer Franklin D. Roosevelt and the title \"Exemption of Edward D. Anderson From Compulsory Retirement for Age\"; the held text concerns Edward D. Anderson and ends with the signature \"FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT\" and the date line \"September 15, 1941.\". Clauses are quoted from that registered file, not from the unregistered clause-drafts copy.\nContent: the order has one recital and one operative sentence. It exempts one named employee, Edward D. Anderson (Chief Clerk, Purchasing Department, The Panama Canal), from compulsory retirement for age under section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), for a period of one year ending September 30, 1942. The recital says he \"will reach the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service\" and asks that he \"be exempted from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year\". The inventory is therefore one clause, the exemption. Extension reading: this order is the initial exemption; any later exemption of the same person would be a new exercise of the section 204 power with its own period and a later event, not an end of this order, and the packet's searches show none in the held texts.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No earlier end found. The only cessation source is the order's own period, \\\"one year ending September 30, 1942\\\". The packet's bounded number search of the held order texts (lookup scope: exactly held_texts(), clause-drafts excluded) found no other held text that cites Executive Order 8899. The Federal Register catalog disposition-note search (later-events:ofr_catalog, checked 2026-10-19) returned no hit for this order. The person-name search of the held order texts (namier:held_texts_person_name, checked 2026-10-28, whose leads list later or undated orders only) found no later order naming Edward D. Anderson. Statute check (the type rule research/readiness/type-rules/TR-RETIREMENT-EXEMPTION.json lists a change in the governing retirement statute as a possible earlier end): held APP exemption orders signed after this one still rest on the same section, for example EO 9271 of November 17, 1942 (\\\"section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), I hereby further exempt the said George T. Summerlin from compulsory retirement for age\\\") and EO 9530 of March 14, 1945 (\\\"section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), I hereby exempt the said Harry F. Hawley from compulsory retirement for age\\\"). Their wording shows that section 204 was still the authority for exemptions after this order's last day, so it had not been repealed. No held source shows that any amendment of section 204 extended or extinguished this order's dated exemption, and a later general rule is an end only with a reason it extinguished the command (owner ruling 2026-10-28). The employee's separation, retirement or death would end the practical use of the exemption but is not an instrument ending the command, and none is cited in the packet. Amendments are later events, not ends, and none is found.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"None found. The order contains one command, the exemption of one named employee; it prescribes no report, procedure, delegation, appropriation or continuing duty. The compulsory-retirement rule it excepts comes from section 204 and the retirement statutes, which are independent of this order, so when the exemption's period ended no command of this order remained to continue.\",\"savings_check\":\"The exemption is exhausted at 1942-09-30; its accrued effects (service, pay, retirement credit) are separate from the order and are stated as transferred_or_accrued. No later exemption order for the same person is found by the name search.\",\"scope_check\":\"Scope: one named employee, Edward D. Anderson (Chief Clerk, Purchasing Department, The Panama Canal). The dossier inventory has one whole-order clause (characters 0-657 of the registered file, the recital and the operative sentence); the signature and date line follow it in the full 697-character text. Nothing in the text is outside that clause, so no inventory correction is needed.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:8899#whole"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:8899#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"4b9b8fefb261e634a85d799c2b4c3541c09bce2643ce1dc01572408799508e98","path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-8899-exemption-edward-d-anderson-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","sha256":"4b9b8fefb261e634a85d799c2b4c3541c09bce2643ce1dc01572408799508e98","state":"match"},"sha256":"127d525501d737888108df22dd2c74794c385105bd1d6dfe13b7e0051bff2468","text_path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-8899-exemption-edward-d-anderson-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","text_sha256":"4b9b8fefb261e634a85d799c2b4c3541c09bce2643ce1dc01572408799508e98"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"4b9b8fefb261e634a85d799c2b4c3541c09bce2643ce1dc01572408799508e98","path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-8899-exemption-edward-d-anderson-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","sha256":"4b9b8fefb261e634a85d799c2b4c3541c09bce2643ce1dc01572408799508e98","state":"match"},"sha256":"127d525501d737888108df22dd2c74794c385105bd1d6dfe13b7e0051bff2468","text_path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-8899-exemption-edward-d-anderson-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","text_sha256":"4b9b8fefb261e634a85d799c2b4c3541c09bce2643ce1dc01572408799508e98"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"4b9b8fefb261e634a85d799c2b4c3541c09bce2643ce1dc01572408799508e98","path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-8899-exemption-edward-d-anderson-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","sha256":"4b9b8fefb261e634a85d799c2b4c3541c09bce2643ce1dc01572408799508e98","state":"match"},"sha256":"127d525501d737888108df22dd2c74794c385105bd1d6dfe13b7e0051bff2468","text_path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-8899-exemption-edward-d-anderson-from-compulsory-retir Page check per jobs/claim-kit/AGENT_PROTOCOL.md (working-text paragraph), done 2026-11-04: the APP mirror page record with the same sha256 in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl (Common Crawl capture 2026-01-19T17:36:48Z) has the heading \"Executive Order 8905—Exemption of Z. Lewis Dalby From Compulsory Retirement for Age\", its date (1941-09-22) and subject agree with the held text, and its text_sha256 equals the held file's. Identity: the register lists this number with date 1941-09-22, signer Franklin D. Roosevelt and the title \"Exemption of Z. Lewis Dalby From Compulsory Retirement for Age\"; the held text concerns Z. Lewis Dalby and ends with the signature \"FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT\" and the date line \"September 22, 1941.\". Clauses are quoted from that registered file, not from the unregistered clause-drafts copy.\nContent: the order has one recital and one operative sentence. It exempts one named employee, Z. Lewis Dalby (Chief Counsel, United States Employees' Compensation Commission), from compulsory retirement for age under section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), for a period of one year ending September 30, 1942. The recital says he \"was exempted from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year by Executive Order No. 8538 of September 12, 1940\" and asks that he \"be further exempted from such compulsory retirement for a period of one year\"; the earlier exemption orders it recites are earlier instruments, not later events, and not ends of this order. The inventory is therefore one clause, the exemption. Extension reading: this order is the further exemption; any later exemption of the same person would be a new exercise of the section 204 power with its own period and a later event, not an end of this order, and the packet's searches show none in the held texts.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No earlier end found. The only cessation source is the order's own period, \\\"one year ending September 30, 1942\\\". The packet's bounded number search of the held order texts (lookup scope: exactly held_texts(), clause-drafts excluded) found no other held text that cites Executive Order 8905. The Federal Register catalog disposition-note search (later-events:ofr_catalog, checked 2026-10-19) returned no hit for this order. The person-name search of the held order texts (namier:held_texts_person_name, checked 2026-10-28, whose leads list later or undated orders only) found no later order naming Z. Lewis Dalby. Statute check (the type rule research/readiness/type-rules/TR-RETIREMENT-EXEMPTION.json lists a change in the governing retirement statute as a possible earlier end): held APP exemption orders signed after this one still rest on the same section, for example EO 9271 of November 17, 1942 (\\\"section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), I hereby further exempt the said George T. Summerlin from compulsory retirement for age\\\") and EO 9530 of March 14, 1945 (\\\"section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), I hereby exempt the said Harry F. Hawley from compulsory retirement for age\\\"). Their wording shows that section 204 was still the authority for exemptions after this order's last day, so it had not been repealed. No held source shows that any amendment of section 204 extended or extinguished this order's dated exemption, and a later general rule is an end only with a reason it extinguished the command (owner ruling 2026-10-28). The employee's separation, retirement or death would end the practical use of the exemption but is not an instrument ending the command, and none is cited in the packet. Amendments are later events, not ends, and none is found.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"None found. The order contains one command, the exemption of one named employee; it prescribes no report, procedure, delegation, appropriation or continuing duty. The compulsory-retirement rule it excepts comes from section 204 and the retirement statutes, which are independent of this order, so when the exemption's period ended no command of this order remained to continue.\",\"savings_check\":\"The exemption is exhausted at 1942-09-30; its accrued effects (service, pay, retirement credit) are separate from the order and are stated as transferred_or_accrued. No later exemption order for the same person is found by the name search.\",\"scope_check\":\"Scope: one named employee, Z. Lewis Dalby (Chief Counsel, United States Employees' Compensation Commission). The dossier inventory has one whole-order clause (characters 0-667 of the registered file, the recital and the operative sentence); the signature and date line follow it in the full 707-character text. Nothing in the text is outside that clause, so no inventory correction is Its only operative sentence exempts William C. Alden from compulsory retirement for age under section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, for a period of one year, ending 1942-09-30. The order states its own term and contains no renewal, conditional, revocation or successor provision, so the exemption ends on its own terms. Evidence: the stated term \"for a period of one year ending September 30, 1942\" is the first end. Recorded inclusive (the exemption runs through the end of 1942-09-30); the exclusive reading, under which it ends when 1942-09-30 begins, is the alternative a reviewer should see (owner ruling 2026-10-21 on until-dates). Provisional text under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21 on transcriptions: original not yet held; replace and re-check quotes when an official copy is retrieved.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Searched for an end earlier than 1942-09-30: a search of every held order text (jobs/*/output/texts excluding clause-drafts, 33118 readable files) for citations of Executive Order No. 8907 found no later order that revokes, supersedes, amends or extends it; also the machine searches of U.S. Code and eCFR citations (0 hits), the Federal Register catalog of disposition notes (0 hits for this order), and the machine search of held order texts for the exempted person's full name (0 hits). No held record shows the employee's separation or death, so no earlier end is shown; the stated date is the first end the held evidence establishes.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order directs no agency, makes no appointment, delegation, appropriation or amendment of any other order, and sets no continuing duty. There is no independent command that could outlast the exemption.\",\"savings_check\":\"State transferred_or_accrued. The order contains no savings, transfer or continuing provision. original not yet held) is a short order dated 1941-12-26. Its only operative sentence exempts Miss Roberta L. Lindsey from compulsory retirement for age under section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, for a period of one year, ending 1942-12-31. The order states its own term and contains no renewal, conditional, revocation or successor provision, so the exemption ends on its own terms. Evidence: the stated term \"for a period of one year ending December 31, 1942\" is the first end. Recorded inclusive (the exemption runs through the end of 1942-12-31); the exclusive reading, under which it ends when 1942-12-31 begins, is the alternative a reviewer should see (owner ruling 2026-10-21 on until-dates). Provisional text under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21 on transcriptions: original not yet held; replace and re-check quotes when an official copy is retrieved. Mendenhall From Compulsory Retirement for Age, date 1942-01-20, and a text_sha256 equal to the held file's; the heading names this order's number and its subject agrees with the text. Clauses are quoted from that registered file, not from the clause-drafts copy. The order (read in full, one paragraph of recital and one operative sentence) recites that the employee was exempted for one year by EO 8628 of January 4, 1941 and further exempts him for one year ending February 28, 1943; the earlier grant is an earlier instrument recited in the text, not a later event, and this order is a new exercise of the section 204 proviso with its own fixed term. First end: the order's own fixed term, recorded as 1943-02-28, inclusive (the exemption runs through the end of that day); the exclusive reading, under which the exemption stops when that day begins, is the alternative. Authority: section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), cited in the order. No held source shows that section repealed or amended before the stated end; a held later exemption order (EO 9530, March 14, 1945) still cites section 204, which corroborates the authority but is not an event affecting this order.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No earlier end, amendment or extension is held. Searches made on 2026-11-04: (1) the packet machine searches current-law:usc and current-law:ecfr (complete, 0 hits for this order), later-events:ofr_catalog (0 hits) and namier:held_texts_person_name (0 hits), selected after reading their coverage notes; (2) a case-insensitive full-text search of every held order text under jobs/*/output/texts and jobs/*/data (clause-drafts excluded) for the employee's name as printed. That search finds this order and, besides it, only EO 8628 (an earlier exemption of the same employee, recited in or preceding this order); no later order names him. (3) a lookup of every other held order text that cites this order by number (Cruden): none. A death or separation of the employee before 1943-02-28 would leave the exemption unused but is not an end of the order on its text, and no held record shows such an event.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order contains one operative command, the time-limited exemption of Walter C. Ingling From Compulsory Retirement for Age, date 1942-01-23, and a text_sha256 equal to the held file's; the heading names this order's number and its subject agrees with the text. Clauses are quoted from that registered file, not from the clause-drafts copy. The order (read in full, one paragraph of recital and one operative sentence) recites that the employee was exempted for one year by EO 8670 of February 4, 1941 and further exempts him for one year ending April 30, 1943; the earlier grant is an earlier instrument recited in the text, not a later event, and this order is a new exercise of the section 204 proviso with its own fixed term. First end: the order's own fixed term, recorded as 1943-04-30, inclusive (the exemption runs through the end of that day); the exclusive reading, under which the exemption stops when that day begins, is the alternative. Authority: section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), cited in the order. No held source shows that section repealed or amended before the stated end; a held later exemption order (EO 9530, March 14, 1945) still cites section 204, which corroborates the authority but is not an event affecting this order.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No earlier end, amendment or extension is held. (3) a lookup of every other held order text that cites this order by number (Cruden): none. Ruggles From Compulsory Retirement for Age, date 1942-01-27, and a text_sha256 equal to the held file's; the heading names this order's number and its subject agrees with the text. Clauses are quoted from that registered file, not from the clause-drafts copy. The order (read in full, one paragraph of recital and one operative sentence) is a first grant: it recites that the employee will reach the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service and exempts him for one year ending January 31, 1943. First end: the order's own fixed term, recorded as 1943-01-31, inclusive (the exemption runs through the end of that day); the exclusive reading, under which the exemption stops when that day begins, is the alternative. Authority: section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), cited in the order. No held source shows that section repealed or amended before the stated end; a held later exemption order (EO 9530, March 14, 1945) still cites section 204, which corroborates the authority but is not an event affecting this order.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No earlier end, amendment or extension is held. A. Elmgren From Compulsory Retirement for Age, date 1942-02-26, and a text_sha256 equal to the held file's; the heading names this order's number and its subject agrees with the text. Clauses are quoted from that registered file, not from the clause-drafts copy. The order (read in full, one paragraph of recital and one operative sentence) is a first grant: it recites that the employee will reach the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service and exempts him for one year ending February 28, 1943. First end: the order's own fixed term, recorded as 1943-02-28, inclusive (the exemption runs through the end of that day); the exclusive reading, under which the exemption stops when that day begins, is the alternative. Authority: section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), cited in the order. No held source shows that section repealed or amended before the stated end; a held later exemption order (EO 9530, March 14, 1945) still cites section 204, which corroborates the authority but is not an event affecting this order.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No earlier end, amendment or extension is held. The clause ended by its own stated period on February 29, 1940, recorded inclusive with the exclusive reading disclosed. Later extension orders are recorded as later events and do not end this order's clause. The text is a transcription (grade mirror_transcription) and the original is not yet held; replace and re-check quotes when an official copy is retrieved.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Candidate earlier ends tested: express revocation, amendment or supersession by a later order; repeal of section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932 before February 29, 1940; and the employee's separation. A repeat search by the producer of all held order texts for this order's number (the forms \\\"No. 8041\\\" and \\\"Executive Order(s) ... 8041\\\") found only these held order texts citing this order by number, each read in full: EO 8328; it is a further exemption of the same employee and does not revoke, amend or supersede this order. The packet's Federal Register disposition-note search (checked 2026-10-19) reported 1 hit for this order (the notes on EO 8328). The person-name search over held order texts (checked 2026-10-28) reported 1 hit (the same extension order). The packet's U.S. Code and eCFR searches (checked 2026-10-17) found no hit for this order. No held source shows a repeal or change of section 204 before February 29, 1940, and a general statute that did not name this order would be only a disclosed candidate under the owner ruling on unnamed instruments, not an end. The held record contains no separation or death date for the employee and none is needed: the permission was stated for a period with a stated ceiling, so the stated end is the earliest end the held evidence supports.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order contains a single operative command, the exemption itself; the recital is the President's stated judgment on which the exemption rests and is not a separate command. The later extension order (EO 8328) is separate later exemptions, not savings or residual effects of this order. The text is a transcription (grade mirror_transcription) and the original is not yet held; replace and re-check quotes when an official copy is retrieved.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Candidate earlier ends tested: express revocation, amendment or supersession by a later order; repeal of section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932 before March 31, 1940; and the employee's separation. The packet's Federal Register disposition-note search (checked 2026-10-19) reported 1 hit for this order (the notes on EO 8345). The person-name search over held order texts (checked 2026-10-28) reported 1 hit (the same extension order). The packet's U.S. Code and eCFR searches (checked 2026-10-17) found no hit for this order. No held source shows a repeal or change of section 204 before March 31, 1940, and a general statute that did not name this order would be only a disclosed candidate under the owner ruling on unnamed instruments, not an end. The held record contains no separation or death date for the employee and none is needed: the permission was stated for a fixed period, so the stated end is the earliest end the held evidence supports.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order contains a single operative command, the exemption itself; the recital is the President's stated judgment on which the exemption rests and is not a separate command. The packet's Federal Register disposition-note search (checked 2026-10-19) reported 1 hit for this order (the notes on EO 8351). The person-name search over held order texts (checked 2026-10-28) reported 1 hit (the same extension order). The packet's U.S. Code and eCFR searches (checked 2026-10-17) found no hit for this order. No held source shows a repeal or change of section 204 before March 31, 1940, and a general statute that did not name this order would be only a disclosed candidate under the owner ruling on unnamed instruments, not an end. The held record contains no separation or death date for the employee and none is needed: the permission was stated for a fixed period, so the stated end is the earliest end the held evidence supports.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order contains a single operative command, the exemption itself; the recital is the President's stated judgment on which the exemption rests and is not a separate command. The text is a transcription (grade mirror_transcription) and the original is not yet held; replace and re-check quotes when an official copy is retrieved.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Candidate earlier ends tested: express revocation, amendment or supersession by a later order; repeal of section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932 before June 30, 1940; and the employee's separation. The packet's U.S. Code and eCFR searches (checked 2026-10-17) found no hit for this order. No held source shows a repeal or change of section 204 before June 30, 1940, and a general statute that did not name this order would be only a disclosed candidate under the owner ruling on unnamed instruments, not an end. The held record contains no separation or death date for the employee and none is needed: the permission was stated for a fixed period, so the stated end is the earliest end the held evidence supports.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order contains a single operative command, the exemption itself; the recital is the President's stated judgment on which the exemption rests and is not a separate command. Larrison from compulsory retirement for age \"for a period of three months ending June 30, 1939\", under section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932 (47 Stat. 382, 404). I read the registered American Presidency Project transcription in full, confirmed its identity against the Common Crawl capture record (number, date, subject heading and text hash agree), and confirmed that it contains one operative command. The clause ended by its own stated period on June 30, 1939, recorded inclusive with the exclusive reading disclosed. No later instrument affecting this order was found in the held corpus. The text is a transcription (grade mirror_transcription) and the original is not yet held; replace and re-check quotes when an official copy is retrieved.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Candidate earlier ends tested: express revocation, amendment or supersession by a later order; repeal of section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932 before June 30, 1939; and the employee's separation. A repeat search by the producer of all held order texts for this order's number (the forms \\\"No. 8074\\\" and \\\"Executive Order(s) ... 8074\\\") found no held order text citing this order by number. The packet's Federal Register disposition-note search (checked 2026-10-19) reported 0 hits for this order. The person-name search over held order texts (checked 2026-10-28) reported 0 hits. The packet's U.S. Code and eCFR searches (checked 2026-10-17) found no hit for this order. No held source shows a repeal or change of section 204 before June 30, 1939, and a general statute that did not name this order would be only a disclosed candidate under the owner ruling on unnamed instruments, not an end. The held record contains no separation or death date for the employee and none is needed: the permission was stated for a fixed period, so the stated end is the earliest end the held evidence supports.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order contains a single operative command, the exemption itself; the recital is the President's stated judgment on which the exemption rests and is not a separate command. The order directs no report, appointment, payment or other act by any official and states no condition, so no independent command survives its stated end.\",\"savings_check\":\"Tested whether any right, duty, proceeding or transition survived the stated end: the order contains none, and service already performed under the exemption is the only accrued effect. The savings source is the order's own text.\",\"scope_check\":\"The command covers one named employee, George K. Larrison, and one stated period; the exemption was not partial in scope, and no held source amends or revokes any part of it. The complete held text (one recital paragraph, one operative paragraph and the signature line) was read, and the inventory of one operative clause is I read the registered American Presidency Project transcription in full, confirmed its identity against the Common Crawl capture record (number, date, subject heading and text hash agree), and confirmed that it contains one operative command. The clause ended by its own stated period on April 30, 1940, recorded inclusive with the exclusive reading disclosed. Later extension orders are recorded as later events and do not end this order's clause. The text is a transcription (grade mirror_transcription) and the original is not yet held; replace and re-check quotes when an official copy is retrieved.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Candidate earlier ends tested: express revocation, amendment or supersession by a later order; repeal of section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932 before April 30, 1940; and the employee's separation. A repeat search by the producer of all held order texts for this order's number (the forms \\\"No. 8094\\\" and \\\"Executive Order(s) ... 8094\\\") found only these held order texts citing this order by number, each read in full: EO 8392; it is a further exemption of the same employee and does not revoke, amend or supersede this order. The packet's Federal Register disposition-note search (checked 2026-10-19) reported 2 hits for this order: a note that EO 8392 amends it and a note that EO 8293 (November 30, 1939) amended it. EO 8392 is the extension order read in full. The held text of EO 8293 is a suspension of the eight-hour law for work at Midway and Wake Islands and does not mention this order or the employee, so the note on EO 8293 is not supported by that text and no effect on this order is shown; it is disclosed here as a rejected earlier candidate (the order's number, the employee's name and the exemption do not appear in its text). The person-name search over held order texts (checked 2026-10-28) reported 1 hit (the same extension order). The packet's U.S. Code and eCFR searches (checked 2026-10-17) found no hit for this order. No held source shows a repeal or change of section 204 before April 30, 1940, and a general statute that did not name this order would be only a disclosed candidate under the owner ruling on unnamed instruments, not an end. The held record contains no separation or death date for the employee and none is needed: the permission was stated for a fixed period, so the stated end is the earliest end the held evidence supports.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order contains a single operative command, the exemption itself; the recital is the President's stated judgment on which the exemption rests and is not a separate command. The order directs no report, appointment, payment or other act by any official and states no condition, so no independent command survives its stated end.\",\"savings_check\":\"Tested whether any right, duty, proceeding or transition survived the stated end: the order contains none, and service already performed under the exemption is the only accrued effect. The later extension order (EO 8392) is separate later exemptions, not savings or residual effects of this order. The savings source is the order's own text.\",\"scope_check\":\"The command covers one named employee, Zeke Johnson, and one stated period; the exemption was not partial in scope, and no held source amends or revokes any part of it. The complete held text (one recital paragraph, one operative paragraph and the signature line) was read, and the inventory of one operative clause is Shambaugh from compulsory retirement for age \"for a period of one year ending August 31, 1940\", under section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932 (47 Stat. 382, 404). I read the registered American Presidency Project transcription in full, confirmed its identity against the Common Crawl capture record (number, date, subject heading and text hash agree), and confirmed that it contains one operative command. The clause ended by its own stated period on August 31, 1940, recorded inclusive with the exclusive reading disclosed. Later extension orders are recorded as later events and do not end this order's clause. The text is a transcription (grade mirror_transcription) and the original is not yet held; replace and re-check quotes when an official copy is retrieved.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Candidate earlier ends tested: express revocation, amendment or supersession by a later order; repeal of section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932 before August 31, 1940; and the employee's separation. A repeat search by the producer of all held order texts for this order's number (the forms \\\"No. 8096\\\" and \\\"Executive Order(s) ... 8096\\\") found only these held order texts citing this order by number, each read in full: EO 8414; it is a further exemption of the same employee and does not revoke, amend or supersede this order. The packet's Federal Register disposition-note search (checked 2026-10-19) reported 1 hit for this order (the notes on EO 8414). The person-name search over held order texts (checked 2026-10-28) reported 1 hit (the same extension order). The packet's U.S. Code and eCFR searches (checked 2026-10-17) found no hit for this order. No held source shows a repeal or change of section 204 before August 31, 1940, and a general statute that did not name this order would be only a disclosed candidate under the owner ruling on unnamed instruments, not an end. The held record contains no separation or death date for the employee and none is needed: the permission was stated for a fixed period, so the stated end is the earliest end the held evidence supports.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order contains a single operative command, the exemption itself; the recital is the President's stated judgment on which the exemption rests and is not a separate command. The order directs no report, appointment, payment or other act by any official and states no condition, so no independent command survives its stated end.\",\"savings_check\":\"Tested whether any right, duty, proceeding or transition survived the stated end: the order contains none, and service already performed under the exemption is the only accrued effect. The later extension order (EO 8414) is separate later exemptions, not savings or residual effects of this order. The savings source is the order's own text.\",\"scope_check\":\"The command covers one named employee, William C. Shambaugh, and one stated period; the exemption was not partial in scope, and no held source amends or revokes any part of it. The complete held text (one recital paragraph, one operative paragraph and the signature line) was read, and the inventory of one operative clause is
